is Hilbert-Schmidt and thus compact from
L^{2}(\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)
into
itself.

(2)

If a function
f\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+},\mathbb{R})
satisfies

|f^{\prime}(x)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle},

then the operator

T_{2}(g)(\eta)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{|\xi-\eta|<1}(f(\xi)-f(\eta))K_{0,1}(\xi,\eta)g(\eta)\eta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\eta

is Hilbert-Schmidt and thus compact from
L^{2}(\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)
into itself.

(3)

If
f
is in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+},\mathbb{R})
satisfies

|f(\eta,\xi)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle\xi\rangle}\qquad\mbox{or}\qquad|f(\eta,\xi)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle\eta\rangle},

then the operator

T_{3}(g)(\eta)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{|\xi-\eta|>1}f(\xi,\eta)K_{0,1}(\xi,\eta)g(\eta)\eta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\eta

is Hilbert-Schmidt and thus compact from
L^{2}(\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)
into itself.

(4)

If a function
f\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+},\mathbb{R})
satisfies

|f(\xi,\eta)|\lesssim\frac{\eta}{\langle\xi\rangle\langle\eta\rangle},

then the operator

T_{4}(g)(\eta)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{|\xi-\eta|<1}f(\xi,\eta)(K_{0,1}(\xi,\eta)+K_{0,1}(\eta,\xi))g(\eta)\eta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\eta

is Hilbert-Schmidt and thus compact from
L^{2}(\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)
into
itself.

Proof.

Step 1. Pointwise estimate on
K_{0,1}
.
We claim that, for
\xi\neq\eta
and
\xi\eta\neq 0
:

|K_{0,1}(\xi,\eta)|\lesssim\frac{|\ln(\max(2,\xi^{-1},\eta^{-1}))|}{|\xi^{2}-\eta^{2}|}.

By definition of the Bessel functions
J_{0}
and
J_{1}

(\Delta+\xi^{2})(J_{0}(\xi r))=\left(\Delta+\eta^{2}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\right)(J_{1}(\eta r))=0,

hence

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}(\Delta+\xi^{2})(J_{0}(\xi r))J_{1}(\eta r)e^{-\varepsilon r}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\xi^{2}K_{0,1}^{\varepsilon}(\xi,\eta)+\int_{0}^{\infty}J_{0}(\xi r)\Delta(J_{1}(\eta r)e^{-\varepsilon r})r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=(\xi^{2}-\eta^{2})K_{0,1}^{\varepsilon}(\xi,\eta)+\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{J_{0}(\xi r)J_{1}(\eta r)}{r^{2}}e^{-\varepsilon r}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\int_{0}^{\infty}J_{0}(\xi r)(J_{1}(\eta r)\Delta(e^{-\varepsilon r})+2\partial_{r}(J_{1}(\eta r))\partial_{r}(e^{-\varepsilon r}))r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r.

(the integrals above are well-defined and the boundary terms vanish since
J_{1}(0)=0
). By the oscillatory nature of
J_{0}
and
J_{1}
we check that for
\eta\xi\neq 0
,

\int_{0}^{\infty}J_{0}(\xi r)(J_{1}(\eta r)\Delta(e^{-\varepsilon r})+2\partial_{r}(J_{1}(\eta r))\partial_{r}(e^{-\varepsilon r}))r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r\rightarrow 0

when
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. As
\varepsilon\to 0
, we find

K_{0,1}(\xi,\eta)=\frac{1}{\eta^{2}-\xi^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{J_{0}(\xi r)J_{1}(\eta r)}{r^{2}}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r.

Using the classical decay estimate on Bessel
functions
|J_{0}(r)|+|J_{1}(r)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle r\rangle^{1/2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{J_{0}(\xi r)J_{1}(\eta r)}{r^{2}}rdr\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\int_{1}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{r\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}\langle\eta r\rangle^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\left|\int_{1}^{\max(\xi^{-1},\eta^{-1})}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{r\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}\langle\eta r\rangle^{1/2}}\right|+\int_{\max(\xi^{-1},\eta^{-1})}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{r\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}\langle\eta r\rangle^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\left|\int_{1}^{\max(\xi^{-1},\eta^{-1})}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{r}\right|+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\xi\eta}}\int_{\max(\xi^{-1},\eta^{-1})}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+|\ln(\max(\xi^{-1},\eta^{-1}))|+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\xi\eta}\max(\xi^{-1},\eta^{-1})}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\ln(\max(2,\xi^{-1},\eta^{-1}))|.

concluding the proof of

|K_{0,1}(\xi,\eta)|\lesssim\frac{|\ln(\max(2,\xi^{-1},\eta^{-1}))|}{|\xi^{2}-\eta^{2}|}.

Step 2. Pointwise estimate on the symmetrized
K_{0,1}
.
We focus here on
|\xi-\eta|\leqslant 1
.
By the formula derived above,

(6.18)

K_{0,1}(\xi,\eta)+K_{0,1}(\eta,\xi)=\frac{1}{\eta^{2}-\xi^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}(J_{0}(\xi r)J_{1}(\eta r)-J_{1}(\xi r)J_{0}(\eta r))\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{r}.

Recall from Lemma
B.2
that

\begin{array}[]{l}J_{0}(r)=\frac{\cos\left(r-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)}{\langle r\rangle^{1/2}}+a_{0}(r)\\
J_{1}(r)=\frac{\sin\left(r-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)}{\langle r\rangle^{1/2}}+a_{1}(r)\end{array}\qquad\mbox{with}\qquad|a_{j}(r)|+\langle r\rangle|a_{j}^{\prime}(r)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle r\rangle^{3/2}},\qquad j=0,1.

Replacing in (
6.18
) the Bessel functions by their leading
orders, we find

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta^{2}-\xi^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\cos\left(\xi r-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}}\frac{\sin\left(\eta r-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)}{\langle\eta r\rangle^{1/2}}-\frac{\cos\left(\eta r-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)}{\langle\eta r\rangle^{1/2}}\frac{\sin\left(\xi r-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}}\right)\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{r}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\frac{1}{\xi+\eta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}\langle\eta r\rangle^{1/2}}\frac{\sin((\eta-\xi)r)}{(\eta-\xi)}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{r}.

and since
|\sin(x)|\lesssim\frac{x}{\langle x\rangle}
we have

\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}\langle\eta r\rangle^{1/2}}\frac{\sin((\eta-\xi)r)}{(\eta-\xi)}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{r}\right|\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}\langle\eta r\rangle^{1/2}\langle|\eta-\xi|r\rangle}.

First, if
\xi,\eta\geqslant 2
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}\langle\eta r\rangle^{1/2}\langle|\eta-\xi|r\rangle}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}\langle\eta r\rangle^{1/2}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\xi\eta}}\int_{1}^{1/|\eta-\xi|}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{r}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\xi\eta}|\eta-\xi|}\int_{1/|\eta-\xi|}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\langle\ln|\xi-\eta|\rangle+\langle\ln\xi\rangle+\langle\ln\eta\rangle}{\sqrt{\xi\eta}},

For
\xi,\eta\leqslant 3
(which cover the full range of
\xi,\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that
|\xi-\eta|\leqslant 1
) it follows from the inequality
\sqrt{\xi\eta}\langle r\rangle\lesssim\langle\eta r\rangle^{1/2}\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}
that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}\langle\eta r\rangle^{1/2}\langle|\eta-\xi|r\rangle}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\xi\eta}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{dr}{\langle r\rangle\langle|\eta-\xi|r\rangle}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\xi\eta}}\left(\int_{0}^{1/|\eta-\xi|}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{\langle r\rangle}+\frac{1}{|\xi-\eta|}\int_{1/|\eta-\xi|}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{r^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\langle\ln(|\xi-\eta|)\rangle}{\sqrt{\xi\eta}}

There remains to deal with the remainder terms. Out of the three terms which involve these remainders, we will focus for the sake of illustration on the following term. Using the bounds on
a_{1}
and
a_{0}
and assuming without loss of generality that
\xi\geq\eta
,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\cos(\xi r-\frac{\pi}{4})}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}}a_{1}(\eta r)-a_{1}(\xi r)\frac{\cos(\eta r-\frac{\pi}{4})}{\langle\eta r\rangle^{1/2}}\right)\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{r}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}}|a_{1}(\eta r)-a_{1}(\xi r)|\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{r}+\int_{0}^{\infty}\left|\frac{\cos(\xi r-\frac{\pi}{4})}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}}-\frac{\cos(\eta r-\frac{\pi}{4})}{\langle\eta r\rangle^{1/2}}\right||a_{1}(\xi r)|\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{r}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}}\left|\frac{1}{\langle\eta r\rangle^{1/2}}-\frac{1}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}}\right|\frac{dr}{r}+\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\frac{|\xi-\eta|r}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}}+\left|\frac{1}{\langle\eta r\rangle^{1/2}}-\frac{1}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}}\right|\right]\frac{1}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{3/2}}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r}{r}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\frac{|\xi-\eta|}{\xi}.

Gathering the above estimates, we find

\left|K_{0,1}(\xi,\eta)+K_{0,1}(\eta,\xi)\right|\lesssim\frac{\langle\ln|\xi-\eta|\rangle+\ln\langle\xi\rangle+\ln\langle\eta\rangle}{(\xi+\eta)\sqrt{\xi\eta}}\qquad\mbox{if $|\xi-\eta|\leq 1$.}

Step 3. The operator
T_{1}
is Hilbert-Schmidt.
It is enough to show that
(f(\xi)-f(\eta))K_{0,1}(\xi,\eta)\in L^{2}(\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\,\eta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\eta,\mathbb{R})
. By Step 1, we have

|(f(\xi)-f(\eta))K_{0,1}(\xi,\eta)|\lesssim\left|\frac{f(\xi)-f(\eta)}{\xi-\eta}\right|\frac{1}{\xi+\eta}

and by the estimates on
f
,

\left|\frac{f(\xi)-f(\eta)}{\xi-\eta}\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle\xi-\eta\rangle}\left(\frac{1}{\langle\xi\rangle}+\frac{1}{\langle\eta\rangle}\right).

Therefore

\frac{1}{\langle\xi-\eta\rangle}\left(\frac{1}{\langle\xi\rangle}+\frac{1}{\langle\eta\rangle}\right)\frac{1}{\xi+\eta}\in L^{2}(\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\,\eta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\eta).

since

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{\langle\xi-\eta\rangle}\frac{1}{\langle\xi\rangle}\frac{1}{\xi+\eta}\right)^{2}\xi\eta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\eta\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\langle\xi-\eta\rangle^{2}}\frac{1}{\langle\xi\rangle^{2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\eta<\infty.

Step 3. The operator
T_{2}
is Hilbert-Schmidt.
Since by Step 1

|(f(\xi)-f(\eta))K_{0,1}(\xi,\eta)|\lesssim\left|\frac{f(\xi)-f(\eta)}{\xi-\eta}\right|\frac{1}{\xi+\eta}

the estimates on
f
give

\mathbf{1}_{|\xi-\eta|<1}|(f(\xi)-f(\eta))K_{0,1}(\xi,\eta)|\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{|\xi-\eta|<1}\frac{1}{\langle\xi\rangle}\frac{1}{\xi+\eta}

and the desired conclusion follows since

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{|\xi-\eta|<1}\left(\frac{1}{\langle\xi\rangle}\frac{1}{\xi+\eta}\right)^{2}\xi\eta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\eta<\infty.

Step 4: The operator
T_{3}
is Hilbert-Schmidt.
By Step 1, it suffices to show that

\frac{1}{\langle\xi\rangle}\frac{\ln(\xi^{-1}+2)+\ln(\eta^{-1}+2)}{|\xi^{2}-\eta^{2}|}\mathbf{1}_{|\xi-\eta|>1}\in L^{2}(\eta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\eta\,\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)

This follows from the observation that

\int_{|\xi-\eta|>1}\frac{\eta\ln(\eta^{-1}+2)^{2}}{(\xi+\eta)^{2}(\xi-\eta)^{2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\eta\lesssim\frac{1}{\xi}\int_{|\xi-\eta|>1}\frac{\ln(\eta^{-1}+2)^{2}}{(\xi-\eta)^{2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\eta\lesssim\frac{1}{\xi},

since then

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\langle\xi\rangle^{2}}\frac{\ln(\xi^{-1}+2)^{2}+\ln(\eta^{-1}+2)^{2}}{|\xi^{2}-\eta^{2}|^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|\xi-\eta|>1}\eta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\eta\,\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\ln(\xi^{-1}+2)^{2}\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle^{2}\xi}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi<\infty.

Step 5: The operator
T_{4}
is Hilbert-Schmidt.

By Step 2, it suffices to check that the following integral is finite:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{|\xi-\eta|<1}\frac{\eta^{2}}{\langle\xi\rangle^{2}\langle\eta\rangle^{2}}\frac{\langle\ln|\xi-\eta|\rangle^{2}+(\ln\langle\xi\rangle)^{2}+(\ln\langle\eta\rangle)^{2}}{(\xi+\eta)^{2}\xi\eta}\eta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\eta\,\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{|\xi-\eta|<1}\frac{\langle\ln|\xi-\eta|\rangle^{2}+(\ln\langle\xi\rangle)^{2}}{\langle\xi\rangle^{4}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\eta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi<\infty.

∎

7.
Estimates on the evolution group

7.1.
L^{2}
estimates for localized solutions

Theorem 7.1

(
L^{2}
growth and boundedness)
.

Let
\epsilon>0
. If
\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{1}
, then for
t\geq 0
we have

\|e^{it\mathcal{H}}\phi\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\sqrt{\log(2+t)}\ \|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{H^{1+\epsilon}}

and if furthermore
\langle\phi,(\rho,\rho)^{\top}\rangle_{L^{2}(r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r)}=0
, then

\|e^{it\mathcal{H}}\phi\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{H^{1+\epsilon}}.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.2
and the semi-group evolution formula
(v)
of Theorem
6.1
we have by decomposing between even and odd parts

\displaystyle\|e^{it\mathcal{H}}\phi\|_{L^{2}(r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r)}

\displaystyle\sim\|(e^{it\mathcal{H}}\phi)_{e}\|_{L^{2}(r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r)}+\|(e^{it\mathcal{H}}\phi)_{o}\|_{L^{2}(r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r)}

\displaystyle\sim\|(e^{it\lambda}\widetilde{\phi})_{e}\|_{L^{2}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}+\|(e^{it\lambda}\widetilde{\phi})_{o}\|_{L^{2}(|\xi|^{-1}\langle\xi\rangle^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}.

The first term satisfies
\|(e^{it\lambda}\widetilde{\phi})_{e}\|_{L^{2}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}\lesssim\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{2}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}
. As for the second term, we further decompose
(e^{it\lambda}\widetilde{\phi})_{o}=\cos(t\lambda)\widetilde{\phi}_{o}+i\sin(t\lambda)\widetilde{\phi}_{e}
.

We have
\widetilde{\phi}_{o}\in L^{2}(|\xi|^{-1}\langle\xi\rangle\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)
by Proposition
5.2
and
\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{C^{\alpha}}\lesssim\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{H^{1+\epsilon}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}
for some
\alpha>0
by Sobolev embedding, so we deduce
\phi_{o}(0)=0
and
|\phi_{o}(\xi)|\lesssim|\xi|^{\alpha}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{H^{1+\epsilon}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}
. Hence the first part is bounded
\|\cos(t\lambda)\widetilde{\phi}_{o}\|_{L^{2}(|\xi|^{-1}\langle\xi\rangle\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}\lesssim\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{H^{1+\epsilon}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}
. We have for the second part that
\widetilde{\phi}_{e}(0)=\langle\phi,(\rho,\rho)^{\top}\rangle_{L^{2}(r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r)}
, so we deduce
\widetilde{\phi}_{e}(\xi)=\langle\phi,(\rho,\rho)^{\top}\rangle_{L^{2}(r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r)}+O(|\xi|^{\alpha}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{H^{1+\epsilon}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)})
. Hence
\|\sin(t\lambda)\widetilde{\phi}_{e}\|_{L^{2}(|\xi|^{-1}\langle\xi\rangle^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}\lesssim\sqrt{1+\ln\langle t\rangle}
unless
\langle\phi,(\rho,\rho)^{\top}\rangle_{L^{2}(r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r)}=0
in which case it is
\lesssim 1
. This implies the desired bounds.
∎

7.2.
Pointwise decay: a model case

We will prove in the next subsection our main decay result (Theorem
7.2
). Before doing so, we shall first estimate a model oscillatory integral which accounts for the contribution of low frequencies in the case where
\widetilde{\phi}
vanishes at the origin.

Lemma 7.1

(Pointwise decay for a model oscillatory integral)
.

Consider

\mathcal{I}_{model}(t,r)=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)W(\xi,r)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi,

where we are denoting

\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)=t\lambda(\xi)-r\xi.

The function
\widetilde{\phi}\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R})
is supported on
|\xi|\lesssim 1
, and for
j=0,1,2
,

(7.1)

|\partial_{\xi}^{j}W(\xi,r)|\lesssim\frac{\langle r\rangle^{j}}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2+j}}.

Then, for
t>1
we have

(7.2)

|\mathcal{I}_{model}(t,r)|\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\qquad\mbox{for }r\geq 0.

Proof.

We will bound the integral on
(0,\infty)
, as the one over
(-\infty,0)
can be estimated by the same arguments, so we restrict

\mathcal{I}_{model}(t,r)=\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)W(\xi,r)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi.

The phase
\Phi_{-}
has a stationary point
\xi_{0}
if
\frac{r}{t}>\sqrt{2}
:

(7.3)

\mbox{if}\;\frac{r}{t}\geq\sqrt{2},\,|\xi|\lesssim 1,\qquad\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi_{0},r)=0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\xi_{0}\sim\left[\frac{r}{t}-\sqrt{2}\right]^{1/2}.

Furthermore, if
0\leq\xi\lesssim 1,\xi\neq 0
, we have

(7.4)

\begin{split}&|\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)|\sim\begin{cases}t&\mbox{if $r\ll t$}\\
r&\mbox{if $r\gg t$}\\
t(\xi^{2}-\xi_{0}^{2})&\mbox{if $r\sim t$}.\end{cases}\\
&|\partial_{\xi}^{2}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)|\sim t\xi.\end{split}

Case 1:
r\gg t
or
t\gg r
. We claim that in this case, under the weaker hypothesis that (
7.1
) is valid for
j=0,1
only, then

(7.5)

|\mathcal{I}_{model}|\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}\|_{H^{1+\epsilon}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}.

Indeed, after an integration by parts,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{model}

\displaystyle=-\int e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\partial_{\xi}(\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi))W(\xi,r)\frac{1}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad-\int e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)\partial_{\xi}\left[W(\xi,r)\right]\frac{1}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad-\int e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)W(\xi,r)\partial_{\xi}\left[\frac{1}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\right]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=I+II+III.

Each of these three terms can now be bounded with the help of the bounds (
7.1
) on
W
, and the lower bound
|\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}|\gtrsim t
valid if
|\xi|\lesssim 1
and
r\gg t
or
t\gg r
:

\displaystyle|I|\lesssim\left\|\partial_{\xi}(\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi))\right\|_{L^{2+\alpha}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}\|\frac{1}{|\xi|^{1/(2+\alpha)}}\frac{1}{t}\frac{1}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}}\|_{L^{\frac{2+\alpha}{1+\alpha}}(|\xi|\lesssim 1,\ \mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\xi\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{1+\epsilon}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}

\displaystyle|II|\lesssim\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{|\xi|\lesssim 1}\frac{1}{t}\frac{\langle r\rangle}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{3/2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle|III|\lesssim\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{|\xi|\lesssim 1}\frac{t\xi}{t^{2}}\frac{1}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}

where for the first inequality we used Hölder with the fact that
\|\partial_{\xi}(\xi\tilde{\phi})\|_{L^{2+\alpha}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}\leq\|\xi\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{1+\epsilon}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}
for some
\alpha>0
by Sobolev embedding.

Case 2:
r\sim t
and
\xi_{0}\lesssim t^{-1/3}
.
We claim that in this case, under the weaker hypothesis that (
7.1
) is valid for
j=0,1
only, then

(7.6)

|\mathcal{I}_{model}|\lesssim\min\left(\frac{1}{t}\left(\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\right)\ ,\ \frac{1}{t^{2/3}}\left(\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}\|_{H^{1+\epsilon}}\right)\right)

To treat this case, we split the integral defining
\mathcal{I}_{model}
as follows

\mathcal{I}_{model}=\int\left[\chi_{-}(c_{0}t^{1/3}\xi)+\chi_{0}(c_{0}t^{1/3}\xi)+\chi_{+}(c_{0}t^{1/3}\xi)\right]\dots\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi=I+II+III,

where the cut-off functions
\chi_{0}
,
\chi_{-}
and
\chi_{+}
are three smooth nonnegative functions which add up to one, and are supported on
[-2,2]
,
(-\infty,-1]
and
[1,\infty)
respectively, and the constant
c_{0}
will be chosen to be sufficiently small for the arguments which follow to apply.

The middle term is the simplest to treat: by the bound (
7.1
) on
W
,

|II|\lesssim\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-Ct^{-1/3}}^{Ct^{-1/3}}|\xi|\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\quad\mbox{or}\quad|II|\lesssim\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{-Ct^{-1/3}}^{Ct^{-1/3}}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2/3}}\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Turning to
III
, it can be written after an integration by parts

\displaystyle III

\displaystyle=-\int e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\partial_{\xi}(\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi))\chi_{+}(c_{0}t^{1/3}\xi)W(\xi,r)\frac{1}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad-\int e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)\chi_{+}(c_{0}t^{1/3}\xi)\partial_{\xi}W(\xi,r)\frac{1}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad-\int e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)\chi_{+}(c_{0}t^{1/3}\xi)W(\xi,r)\partial_{\xi}\left[\frac{1}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\right]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad-\int e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)\partial_{\xi}[\chi_{+}(c_{0}t^{1/3}\xi)]W(\xi,r)\frac{1}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=III_{1}+III_{2}+III_{3}+III_{4}.

We now choose
c_{0}
such that
\xi>5\xi_{0}
on the support of the above integrand, which implies that
|\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}|\gtrsim t\xi^{2}
. Using this lower bound in conjunction with the estimate (
7.1
) on
W
, and keeping in mind that
r\sim t
, for
\alpha\in(0,\infty]
we have

\displaystyle|III_{1}|\lesssim\|\partial_{\xi}(\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi))\|_{L^{2+\alpha}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}\left\|\chi_{+}\left(c_{0}t^{1/3}\xi\right)\frac{1}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{1}{t\xi^{2}}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2+\alpha}{1+\alpha}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{\frac{2}{3}+\frac{\alpha}{2+\alpha}}}\|\partial_{\xi}(\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi))\|_{L^{2+\alpha}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}.

Hence, taking either
\alpha=+\infty
or
\alpha>0
small (depending on
\varepsilon
), we have

|III_{1}|\lesssim\min\left(\frac{1}{t}\|\partial_{\xi}(\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi))\|_{L^{\infty}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}\ ,\frac{1}{t^{2/3}}\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)\|_{H^{1+\epsilon}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}\right),

where we used Sobolev embedding for the second inequality. Similarly

\displaystyle|III_{2}|\lesssim\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\xi\gtrsim t^{-1/3}}\frac{1}{t\xi^{2}}\xi\frac{\langle r\rangle}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{3/2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\quad\mbox{or}\quad|III_{2}|\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2/3}}\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle|III_{3}|\lesssim\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\xi\gtrsim t^{-1/3}}\frac{t\xi}{(t\xi^{2})^{2}}\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\quad\mbox{or}\quad|III_{3}|\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2/3}}\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle|III_{4}|\lesssim\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\xi\approx t^{-1/3}}\frac{t^{1/3}}{t\xi^{2}}\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\quad\mbox{or}\quad|III_{3}|\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2/3}}\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Finally,
I
can be estimated in a similar way to
III
.

Case 3:
r\sim t
and
\xi_{0}\gg t^{-1/3}
.

We introduce the new scale
R=(t\xi_{0})^{-1/2}
; notice that
R\ll\xi_{0}
since
\xi_{0}\gg t^{-1/3}
. The integral
\mathcal{I}_{model}
is now split as follows

\mathcal{I}_{model}=\int\left[\chi_{-}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)+\chi_{0}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)+\chi_{+}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)\right]\dots\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi=I+II+III,

The middle term can be bounded immediately:

|II|\lesssim\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{\xi_{0}R}{\langle\xi_{0}r\rangle^{1/2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}},

but the right term requires an integration by parts

\displaystyle III

\displaystyle=-\int e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\partial_{\xi}\left[\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)\right]\chi_{+}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)W(\xi,r)\frac{1}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad-\int e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)\frac{1}{R}\chi_{+}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)W(\xi,r)\frac{1}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad-\int e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)\chi_{+}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)\partial_{\xi}W(\xi,r)\frac{1}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad-\int e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)\chi_{+}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)W(\xi,r)\partial_{\xi}\left[\frac{1}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\right]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=III_{1}+III_{2}+III_{3}+III_{4}.

To estimate
III_{1}
, we do another integration by parts:

\displaystyle III_{1}

\displaystyle=-\int e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\partial_{\xi}\left[\partial_{\xi}(\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi))\chi_{+}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)\right]W(\xi,r)\frac{1}{(\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r))^{2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad-\int e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\partial_{\xi}\left[\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)\right]\chi_{+}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)\partial_{\xi}W(\xi,r)\frac{1}{(\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r))^{2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad-\int e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\partial_{\xi}\left[\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)\right]\chi_{+}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)W(\xi,r)\partial_{\xi}\left[\frac{1}{(\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r))^{2}}\right]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=III_{1,1}+III_{1,2}+III_{1,3}.

First of all, distributing the derivatives in
III_{1,1}
, we get

\displaystyle|III_{1,1}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{W^{2,\infty}}\int\left(\chi_{+}\big(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\big)+\frac{1}{R}|\chi_{+}^{\prime}\big(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\big)|\right)\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}t^{2}(\xi^{2}-\xi_{0}^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{W^{2,\infty}}\left(\frac{1}{t^{2}r^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi_{0}^{\frac{5}{2}}R}+\frac{1}{t^{2}r^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi_{0}^{\frac{5}{2}}R^{2}}\right)\ \lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{W^{2,\infty}}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle|III_{1,2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\int\chi_{+}\big(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\big)\frac{r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}t^{2}(\xi^{2}-\xi_{0}^{2})^{2}}\lesssim\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\frac{1}{t^{2}r^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi_{0}^{\frac{7}{2}}R}\ \lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}

and

\displaystyle|III_{1,3}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\int\chi_{+}\big(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\big)\frac{|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}t^{2}(\xi^{2}-\xi_{0}^{2})^{3}}\lesssim\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\frac{1}{t^{2}r^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi_{0}^{\frac{5}{2}}R^{2}}\ \lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}

We now claim that, under the weaker hypothesis that (
7.1
) is valid for
j=0,1
only, then

(7.7)

|III_{2}|+|III_{3}|+|III_{4}|\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Let us now prove (
7.7
). The first term is estimated as above,

\displaystyle|III_{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int\frac{1}{R}\chi_{+}^{\prime}\big(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\big)\frac{|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}t(\xi^{2}-\xi_{0}^{2})}\lesssim\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{1}{tr^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}R}\ \lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

The third term
III_{3}
is the most delicate: it can be bounded by

|III_{3}|\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\xi>\xi_{0}+CR}\frac{\xi}{\xi^{2}-\xi_{0}^{2}}\frac{\langle r\rangle}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{3/2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}

where we estimated the above integral as follows

\displaystyle\int_{\xi>\xi_{0}+CR}\frac{\xi}{\xi^{2}-\xi_{0}^{2}}\frac{\langle r\rangle}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{3/2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\xi_{0}+CR}^{2\xi_{0}}\frac{1}{\xi_{0}}\frac{1}{\xi-\xi_{0}}\xi_{0}\frac{1}{\xi_{0}^{3/2}r^{1/2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{2\xi_{0}}^{C}\frac{1}{\xi^{2}}\xi\frac{1}{\xi^{3/2}r^{1/2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\lesssim\xi_{0}^{-3/2}r^{-1/2}\langle\log(\frac{R}{\xi_{0}})\rangle+\xi_{0}^{-3/2}r^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\xi_{0}^{-3/2}r^{-1/2}\langle\log(\xi_{0}^{-3/2}r^{-1/2})\rangle+\xi_{0}^{-3/2}r^{-1/2}\lesssim 1,

where we used that
\xi_{0}^{-3/2}r^{-1/2}\sim\xi_{0}^{-3/2}t^{-1/2}\lesssim 1
. Finally,

|III_{4}|\lesssim\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\xi>\xi_{0}+CR}\frac{\xi}{t(\xi^{2}-\xi_{0}^{2})^{2}}\frac{\xi}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}},

after estimating the integral upon splitting it into the contributions of
\xi_{0}+CR<\xi<2\xi_{0}
and
\xi>2\xi_{0}
.

There remains to estimate
I
. We proceed just like for
III
and integrate by parts (noticing that there is no boundary term since
(\xi\widetilde{\phi})(0)=0
). We obtain terms
I_{1}
,
I_{2}
,
I_{3}
,
I_{4}
which only differ from
III_{1}
,
III_{2}
,
III_{3}
,
III_{4}
in that
\chi_{+}
is replaced by
\chi_{-}
. The last three terms can then be estimated in a similar way:

\displaystyle|I_{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int\frac{1}{R}\chi_{-}^{\prime}\big(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\big)\frac{|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}t(\xi^{2}-\xi_{0}^{2})}\lesssim\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{1}{tr^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}R}\ \lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}},

\displaystyle|I_{3}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{\xi_{0}-CR}\frac{\xi}{\xi_{0}^{2}-\xi^{2}}\frac{1}{\xi^{3/2}r^{1/2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\left[\xi_{0}^{-3/2}r^{-1/2}\langle\log(\xi_{0}^{-3/2}r^{-1/2})\rangle+\xi_{0}^{-3/2}r^{-1/2}\right]\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}},

\displaystyle|I_{4}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{\xi_{0}-CR}\frac{\xi}{t(\xi^{2}-\xi_{0}^{2})^{2}}\frac{\xi}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}},

and there only remains to estimate the first term that we decompose as follows in order to avoid a boundary term in an integration by parts later

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\partial_{\xi}\left[\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)\right]\chi_{0}\left(\frac{\xi}{c_{0}t^{-1/3}}\right)W(\xi,r)\frac{1}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\partial_{\xi}\left[\xi\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)\right]\chi_{+}\left(\frac{\xi}{c_{0}t^{-1/3}}\right)\chi_{-}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)W(\xi,r)\frac{1}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=I_{1}^{1}+I_{1}^{2}

for
c_{0}
a small constant. The first term is estimated directly:

|I_{1}^{1}|\lesssim\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\int_{0}^{2c_{0}t^{-1/3}}\frac{1}{r^{1/2}\xi^{1/2}}\frac{1}{t(\xi_{0}^{2}-\xi^{2})}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\frac{1}{t^{7/6}r^{1/2}\xi_{0}^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}

since
\xi_{0}\gg t^{-1/3}
. We integrate by parts for the second term and obtain terms
I_{1,1}^{2}
,
I_{1,2}^{2}
and
I_{1,3}^{2}
which are the analogues of
III_{1,1}
,
III_{1,2}
and
III_{1,3}
before up to replacing
\chi_{+}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)
by
\chi_{+}\left(\frac{\xi}{c_{0}t^{-1/3}}\right)\chi_{-}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)
. They are estimated exactly as before, namely:

\displaystyle|I_{1,1}^{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{W^{2,\infty}}\int\left(\chi_{-}\big(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\big)+\frac{1}{R}|\chi_{-}^{\prime}\big(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\big)+t^{1/3}|\chi_{0}^{\prime}\big(\frac{\xi}{c_{0}t^{1/3}}\big)|\right)\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}t^{2}(\xi^{2}-\xi_{0}^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{W^{2,\infty}}\left(\frac{1}{t^{2}r^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi_{0}^{\frac{5}{2}}R}+\frac{1}{t^{2}r^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi_{0}^{\frac{5}{2}}R^{2}}+\frac{1}{t^{7/3}\xi_{0}^{4}}\right)\ \lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{W^{2,\infty}}

\displaystyle|I_{1,2}^{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\int\chi_{-}\big(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\big)\frac{r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}t^{2}(\xi^{2}-\xi_{0}^{2})^{2}}\lesssim\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\frac{1}{t^{2}r^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi_{0}^{\frac{7}{2}}R}\ \lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{W^{1,\infty}},

\displaystyle|I_{1,3}^{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\int\chi_{-}\big(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\big)\frac{|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}t^{2}(\xi^{2}-\xi_{0}^{2})^{3}}\lesssim\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\frac{1}{t^{2}r^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi_{0}^{\frac{5}{2}}R^{2}}\ \lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}.

This concludes the proof of (
7.2
).
∎

7.3.
Pointwise decay: the main result

Theorem 7.2

(Pointwise decay)
.

Let
\epsilon>0
and
\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{1}
. If
0<t\leqslant 1
, we have

\|e^{it\mathcal{H}}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\left\|\widetilde{\phi}\right\|_{H^{1+\epsilon}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}.

If
t>1,
then

(7.8)

\|e^{it\mathcal{H}}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim_{\epsilon}\frac{1}{t^{2/3}}\left\|\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)\right\|_{H^{1+\epsilon}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)},

and if
\langle\phi,(\rho,\rho)^{\top}\rangle_{L^{2}(r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r)}=0
,

(7.9)

\|e^{it\mathcal{H}}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim_{\epsilon}\frac{1}{t}\left\|\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)\right\|_{H^{2+\epsilon}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}.

Proof.

We start from the formula

e^{it\mathcal{H}}\phi=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{it\lambda(\xi)}\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)\psi(\xi,r)\lambda^{\prime}(\xi)\operatorname{sign}\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi,

which we split into low and high frequencies (the cutoff functions

\chi_{0}
,
\chi_{-}
and
\chi_{+}
being three smooth nonnegative functions which add up to one, and are supported on
[-2,2]
,
(-\infty,-1]
and
[1,\infty)
respectively)

e^{it\mathcal{H}}\phi=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}[\chi_{0}(\xi)+\chi_{+}(\xi)+\chi_{-}(\xi)]\dots\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi=I_{lo}+I_{hi,+}+I_{hi,-}.

Case 1: high frequencies
\xi\gtrsim 1
.
We claim that for
t>0
, we have

|I_{hi,-}|+|I_{hi,+}|\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{H^{1+\epsilon}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)},

and now prove this estimate. Since the terms
I_{hi,+}
and
I_{hi,-}
can be treated identically, we focus on the former, which corresponds to
\xi\gtrsim 1
.
By Theorem
1.4
and Appendix
B
, it can be written

(7.10)

I_{hi,+}=\sum_{\pm}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{i\Phi_{\pm}(t,\xi,r)}\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)W_{\pm}(\xi,r)a(\xi)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

(note that we do not need to distinguish the remainder term in Theorem
1.4
, it can be merged with the main term). Here, we abbreviated

a(\xi)=\lambda^{\prime}(\xi)\operatorname{sign}(\xi)\chi_{+}(\xi),\qquad\Phi_{\pm}(t,\xi,r)=\lambda(\xi)t\pm\xi r,

and the functions
W_{\pm}
and
a
satisfy the estimates for
|\xi|\gtrsim 1

(7.11)

\begin{split}&|W_{\pm}(\xi,r)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}},\qquad|\partial_{\xi}W_{\pm}(\xi,r)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\xi\langle r\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\\
&|a(\xi)|\lesssim|\xi|,\quad|a^{\prime}(\xi)|\lesssim 1.\end{split}

Since the phase
\Phi_{+}
is never stationary for
t,\xi\gtrsim 1
, we shall focus on the phase
\Phi_{-}
. Its derivatives and its stationary point
\xi_{0}
are given by

\displaystyle\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)=t\lambda^{\prime}(\xi)-r,\qquad\partial_{\xi}^{2}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)=t\lambda^{\prime\prime}(\xi)

\displaystyle\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi_{0},r)=0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\lambda^{\prime}(\xi_{0})=\frac{r}{t}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\xi_{0}\sim\frac{r}{t}\quad\mbox{if $|\xi_{0}|\gtrsim 1$}.

Furthermore, if
\xi_{0}\gtrsim 1
or
\xi\gtrsim 1
and
\xi_{0}\ll 1
,

(7.12)

\begin{split}|\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)|\sim t|\xi-\xi_{0}|,\qquad|\partial_{\xi}^{2}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)|\sim t.\end{split}

We now come back to formula (
7.10
) and, as explained above, we only keep the summand involving the
-
phase: we denote

\mathcal{I}_{hi,+}=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)W_{-}(\xi,r)a(\xi)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi.

Case 1.1:
\xi\gtrsim 1
and
r\ll t
.
Integrating by parts gives the following terms

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{hi,+}

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)W_{-}(\xi,r)a(\xi)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=-\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\partial_{\xi}\left(\frac{W_{-}(\xi,r)a(\xi)}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\right)\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi-\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\frac{W_{-}(\xi,r)a(\xi)}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\partial_{\xi}\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi.

Since
\xi\gtrsim 1
and
r\ll t
, we see that
|\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}|\sim t\xi
, so that, by the bounds (
7.11
) on
W_{-}
and
a
,

\left|\frac{W_{-}(\xi,r)a(\xi)}{\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{t\xi\langle r\xi\rangle^{1/2}},\qquad\left|\partial_{\xi}\left(\frac{W_{-}(\xi,r)a(\xi)}{\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\right)\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{t\xi\langle r\xi\rangle^{1/2}}.

Therefore, we can bound

\left|I_{hi,+}\right|\lesssim_{\epsilon}\frac{1}{t}\left[\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{2}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}+\|\partial_{\xi}\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{2}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}\right]

Case 1.2:
\xi\gtrsim 1
and
r\gtrsim t
. In that case, we need to split the integral further in order to isolate the stationary point. Recall that
\chi_{0}
,
\chi_{-}
and
\chi_{+}
be three smooth nonnegative cutoff functions which add up to one, and are supported on
[-2,2]
,
(-\infty,-1]
and
[1,\infty)
respectively. We write then

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)W_{-}(\xi,r)a(\xi)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=\int\left[\chi_{-}(\sqrt{t}(\xi-\xi_{0}))+\chi_{0}(\sqrt{t}(\xi-\xi_{0}))+\chi_{+}(\sqrt{t}(\xi-\xi_{0}))\right]\dots\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=I+II+III.

The middle piece
II
is estimated in a straightforward manner: by the bounds (
7.11
) on
a
and
W_{-}
and since
\xi_{0}\sim\frac{r}{t}
,

|II|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\left\|\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)W_{-}(\xi,r)a(\xi)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\frac{\xi_{0}}{\langle r\xi_{0}\rangle^{1/2}}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

To deal with
I
, we integrate by parts and get

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=-\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\partial_{\xi}\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)W_{-}(\xi,r)a(\xi)\chi_{-}(\sqrt{t}(\xi-\xi_{0}))\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)\partial_{\xi}[W_{-}(\xi,r)a(\xi)]\chi_{-}(\sqrt{t}(\xi-\xi_{0}))\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\widetilde{\phi}(\xi)W_{-}(\xi,r)a(\xi)\partial_{\xi}\left[\frac{\chi_{-}(\sqrt{t}(\xi-\xi_{0}))}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\right]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}.

Using successively the bounds (
7.11
) and (
7.12
) and the fact that
\|\frac{1}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{1/2}|\xi-\xi_{0}|}\|_{L^{2}(1\lesssim\xi\leq\xi_{0}-\frac{C}{\sqrt{t}},\ \xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}\lesssim 1
, we have by Cauchy-Schwarz

\displaystyle|I_{1}|\lesssim\int_{1\lesssim\xi\leq\xi_{0}-\frac{C}{\sqrt{t}}}\frac{1}{t|\xi-\xi_{0}|}|\partial_{\xi}\widetilde{\phi}|\frac{\xi}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\left\|\partial_{\xi}\widetilde{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}.

The term
I_{2}
is lower order: indeed,

\displaystyle|I_{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{1\lesssim\xi\leq\xi_{0}-\frac{C}{\sqrt{t}}}\frac{1}{t|\xi-\xi_{0}|}\frac{1}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\frac{\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}}{t\langle r\rangle^{1/2}}\int_{1\lesssim\xi\leq\xi_{0}-\frac{C}{\sqrt{t}}}\frac{1}{|\xi-\xi_{0}|}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\langle\log t\rangle+\langle\log r\rangle}{t\langle r\rangle^{1/2}}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

We finally come to
I_{3}
, which can be bounded as follows

|I_{3}|\lesssim\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{1\lesssim\xi\leq\xi_{0}-\frac{C}{\sqrt{t}}}\frac{\xi}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\frac{1}{t|\xi-\xi_{0}|^{2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\frac{\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}}{t^{3/2}}\int_{1\lesssim\xi\leq\xi_{0}-\frac{C}{\sqrt{t}}}\frac{1}{|\xi-\xi_{0}|^{2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Similarly,
III
can be written as
III_{1}+III_{2}+III_{3}
(the only difference in the formulas being the replacement of
\chi_{-}
by
\chi_{+}
); these terms can then be estimated similarly to
I_{1}
,
I_{2}
and
I_{3}
. For the term
III_{1}
, we use that

\|\frac{1}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{1/2}|\xi-\xi_{0}|}\|_{L^{2}(\xi\geq\xi_{0}+\frac{C}{\sqrt{t}},\ |\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}\lesssim 1
to obtain that

\displaystyle|III_{1}|\lesssim\int_{\xi\geq\xi_{0}+\frac{C}{\sqrt{t}}}\frac{1}{t|\xi-\xi_{0}|}|\partial_{\xi}\widetilde{\phi}|\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\left\|\partial_{\xi}\widetilde{\phi}\right\|_{L^{2}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}.

The term
|III_{2}|
is lower order

\displaystyle|III_{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\xi\geq\xi_{0}+\frac{C}{\sqrt{t}}}\frac{1}{t|\xi-\xi_{0}|}\frac{1}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\frac{\langle\log t\rangle}{t\langle r\rangle^{1/2}}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}

and finally,

|III_{3}|\lesssim\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\xi\geq\xi_{0}+\frac{C}{\sqrt{t}}}\frac{\xi}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}}\frac{1}{t|\xi-\xi_{0}|^{2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{t^{1/2}\xi_{0}^{1/2}+\xi_{0}^{-1/2}}{tr^{1/2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Case 2: low frequencies
|\xi|\lesssim 1
with a cancellation at zero frequency.
For high frequencies which were the object of Case 1, the vanishing of the distorted Fourier transform at frequency zero is obviously irrelevant; but low frequency estimates will be responsible for the different decay rates in (
7.9
) and (
7.8
). For now, we will prove (
7.9
), whose right-hand side is finite if
\widetilde{\phi}
vanishes suitably at
0
; the proof of (
7.8
) will be the object of Case 3 below.

In order to use the decomposition (
1.3
), we decompose the Bessel function as

J_{0}(r)=W_{+,1}e^{ir}+W_{-,1}e^{-ir}

where
|\partial_{r}^{j}W_{\pm,1}|\lesssim r^{-j}
(see Appendix
B
) and further introduce

\displaystyle W_{+,2}=(\rho(r)-1)e^{i\xi r}\chi_{\xi^{-1}}(r),\qquad W_{-,2}=0,

\displaystyle W_{+,3}=\frac{e^{-i\pi/4}}{2i\sqrt{\xi r}}(1-\chi_{\xi^{-1}}(r)),\qquad W_{-,3}=-\frac{e^{i\pi/4}}{2i\sqrt{\xi r}}(1-\chi_{\xi^{-1}}(r)),

\displaystyle W_{+,4}=\frac{1}{\xi}m_{\flat}^{\textup{loc}}\chi_{\xi^{-1}}(r)e^{-i\xi r},\qquad W_{-,4}=0

\displaystyle W_{+,5}=\frac{1}{2\xi}(m^{R}_{\flat,1}-im^{R}_{\flat,2}),\qquad W_{-,5}=\frac{1}{2\xi}(m^{R}_{\flat,1}+im^{R}_{\flat,2}),

and

(7.13)

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{1}=\tilde{\phi}_{2}=\frac{\tilde{\phi}}{\xi}b_{\flat}\lambda^{\prime}\chi_{0}(\xi)e(\xi)\textup{sign}\xi,\quad\tilde{\phi}_{3}=\frac{\tilde{\phi}}{\xi}c_{\flat}\lambda^{\prime}\chi_{0}(\xi)e(\xi)\textup{sign}\xi,\quad\tilde{\phi}_{4}=\tilde{\phi}_{5}=\tilde{\phi}\lambda^{\prime}\chi_{0}(\xi)\textup{sign}\xi,

so that we have

I_{lo}=\sum_{\pm}\sum_{n=1}^{5}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}e^{i\Phi_{\pm}(t,\xi,r)}\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\xi)W_{\pm,n}(\xi,r)\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi.

We recall that
\lambda^{\prime}
is
C^{\infty}
near the origin, and remark that for all
n\in\{1,5\}
,

(7.14)

\begin{split}&|W_{\pm,n}(\xi,r)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}}\qquad|\partial_{\xi}W_{\pm,n}(\xi,r)|\lesssim\frac{\langle r\rangle}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{3/2}}.\end{split}

Turning to the stationary phase analysis, we can focus without loss of generality on the
-
case in
I_{lo}
, as in the
+
case there is no stationary point so that estimates are much easier, and we restrict the integral to
(0,\infty)
as the integral over
(-\infty,0)
can be estimated similarly: we will bound the term

(7.15)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{lo}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}(\widetilde{\phi}_{1}(\xi)W_{-,1}(\xi,r)+\widetilde{\phi}_{3}(\xi)W_{-,3}(\xi,r)+\widetilde{\phi}_{5}(\xi)W_{-,5}(\xi,r))\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1,3,5}\mathcal{I}_{lo,n}.

We recall that the stationary point
\xi_{0}
of the phase
\Phi_{-}
and its derivatives satisfy (
7.3
)-(
7.4
). We will bound
\mathcal{I}_{lo,n}
for
n=1,3,5
relying on Lemma
7.1
which estimates a model integral for
\mathcal{I}_{lo}
: it will either be applied directly, or in cases where it will not apply directly since
\tilde{\phi}_{n}
is not
C^{2}
(the coefficients
b_{\flat}
and
c_{\flat}
having a
\xi^{2}\ln^{2}\xi
singularity at the origin) its proof will be adapted to handle the present case.

Case 2.0:
0\leqslant t<2
. In that case, we simply remark that
\mathcal{I}_{lo}
is the integral of a bounded function over
\{|\xi|\lesssim 1\}
, so that

|\mathcal{I}_{lo}|\lesssim\sum_{n=1,3,5}\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Therefore, we now assume
t\geqslant 2
in all forthcoming cases.

Case 2.1:
r\gg t
or
t\gg r
, or
r\sim t
and
\xi_{0}\lesssim t^{-1/3}
. In that case, we have directly by applying (
7.5
) and (
7.6
) to each of the three terms in (
7.15
) that

|\mathcal{I}_{lo}|\lesssim\min\left(\frac{1}{t}\left(\|\frac{\widetilde{\phi}}{\xi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\right)\ ,\ \frac{1}{t^{2/3}}\left(\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{H^{1+\epsilon}}\right)\right).

Case 2.2:
r\sim t
and
\xi_{0}\gg t^{-1/3}
.
As in the proof of Lemma
7.1
, we introduce the scale
R=(t\xi_{0})^{-1/2}
and recall that
R\ll\xi_{0}
since
\xi_{0}\gg t^{-1/3}
. For
n=3
and
n=5
the integral
\mathcal{I}_{lo,n}
is now split as follows

(7.16)

\mathcal{I}_{lo,n}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\chi_{-}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)+\chi_{0}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)+\chi_{+}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)\right]\dots\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi=I_{n}+II_{n}+III_{n},

The middle term can be bounded immediately, using (
7.14
) and
|\tilde{\phi}_{n}|\lesssim|\tilde{\phi}|
for
n=3,5
(because
|c_{\flat}|\lesssim|\xi|^{2}|\log^{2}\xi|
from (
1.5
)):

|II_{n}|\lesssim\|\widetilde{\phi}_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{\xi_{0}R}{\langle\xi_{0}r\rangle^{1/2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

We integrate by parts the right-term

\displaystyle III_{n}

\displaystyle=-\int e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\partial_{\xi}(\xi\widetilde{\phi}_{n})(\xi)\chi_{+}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)W_{-,n}(\xi,r)\frac{1}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad-\int e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\xi)\partial_{\xi}\left[\chi_{+}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)\right]W_{-,n}(\xi,r)\frac{1}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad-\int e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\xi)\chi_{+}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)\partial_{\xi}\left[W_{-,n}(\xi,r)\right]\frac{1}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\qquad-\int e^{i\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\xi)\chi_{+}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)W_{-,n}(\xi,r)\partial_{\xi}\left[\frac{1}{i\partial_{\xi}\Phi_{-}(t,\xi,r)}\right]\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=III_{n,1}+III_{n,2}+III_{n,3}+III_{n,4}.

We bound the first term using that for
n=3,5
we have
|\partial_{\xi}(\xi\tilde{\phi}_{n})|\lesssim|\xi|(\||\xi|^{-1}\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{\xi}\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}})
(because
|\partial_{\xi}^{j}c_{\flat}|\lesssim|\xi|^{2-j}|\log^{2}\xi|
for
j=0,1
from (
1.5
)):

\displaystyle|III_{n,1}|\lesssim

\displaystyle(\||\xi|^{-1}\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{\xi}\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}})\int\chi_{+}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)\frac{\xi}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{1}{t(\xi^{2}-\xi_{0}^{2})}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\ln R}{tr^{1/2}\xi_{0}^{1/2}}(\||\xi|^{-1}\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{\xi}\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}})\ \lesssim\frac{1}{t}(\||\xi|^{-1}\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{\xi}\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}})

because
(\xi_{0}t)^{-1/2}\ln R=R\ln R\ll 1
. We apply (
7.7
) to the last four terms and obtain:

|III_{n,2}+III_{n,3}+III_{n,4}|\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\||\xi|^{-1}\tilde{\phi}_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Combining, we get

|III_{n}|\lesssim\frac{1}{t}(\||\xi|^{-1}\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{\xi}\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}})

The contribution of
I_{n}
for
n=3,5
can be estimated similarly to
III_{n}
, leading to

|I_{n}|\lesssim\frac{1}{t}(\||\xi|^{-1}\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{\xi}\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}).

Hence, for
n=3,5
we have

|\mathcal{I}_{lo,n}|\lesssim\frac{1}{t}(\||\xi|^{-1}\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{\xi}\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}).

There remains to estimate
\mathcal{I}_{lo,1}
. To do so, recall that this part bounds the contribution of leading order part of
\psi^{S}_{\flat}
consisting of the Bessel function
J_{0}
, so that we need to bound

\mathcal{I}_{lo,1}=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{it\lambda(\xi)}\widetilde{\phi}_{1}(\xi)J_{0}(\xi r)\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi.

We rely on Fourier analysis, recognizing that this is the two-dimensional inverse Fourier transform in the radial sector of
\xi\mapsto e^{it\lambda(\xi)}\widetilde{\phi}_{1}(\xi)
, and hence using that
\widetilde{\phi}_{1}
is supported in
\{|\xi|\leq 2\}
and that Fourier transform exchanges products and convolution,

\mathcal{I}_{lo,1}=K(t,\cdot)*\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\widetilde{\phi}_{1})

where, up to a constant,

K(t,r)=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{it\lambda(\xi)}\chi_{0}(\xi/2)J_{0}(\xi r)\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi.

Appealing to Lemma
7.1
we have
\|K(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim t^{-1}
so that

|\mathcal{I}_{lo,1}|\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\widetilde{\phi}_{1})\|_{L^{1}}.

We pick
0<\epsilon^{\prime}<\epsilon
and estimate using the two-dimensional embedding
L^{2,1+\epsilon^{\prime}}\subset L^{1}
and continuity
\mathcal{F}^{-1}:H^{1+\epsilon^{\prime}}\rightarrow L^{2,1+\epsilon^{\prime}}
that
\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\widetilde{\phi}_{1})\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\|\widetilde{\phi}_{1}\|_{H^{1+\epsilon^{\prime}}(|\xi|d\xi)}
. By the definition (
7.13
) of
\widetilde{\phi}_{1}
we have
\|\widetilde{\phi}_{1}\|_{H^{1+\epsilon}(|\xi|d\xi)}\approx\|\frac{1}{\xi}\widetilde{\phi}\|_{H^{1+\epsilon}(|\xi|d\xi)}
. We then recall that since
\epsilon>\epsilon^{\prime}
, then for functions
f\in H^{2+\epsilon^{\prime}}
such that
f(0)=0
, we have
\|\frac{1}{\xi}f\|_{H^{1+\epsilon^{\prime}}(|\xi|d\xi)}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{2+\epsilon}(|\xi|d\xi)}
. Hence

|\mathcal{I}_{lo,1}|\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{H^{2+\epsilon}(|\xi|d\xi)}.

Case 3: low frequencies
|\xi|\lesssim 1
without cancellation at zero frequency.
We now aim at proving (
7.8
), but it turns out that the necessary modifications in Case 2 are small. Case 2.1. apply verbatim; as for case 2.3, the same scheme of proof can be followed up to small adaptations. Namely, we consider
r\sim t
and
\xi_{0}\gg t^{-1/3}
and keep the notations of case 2.3. We decompose
\mathcal{I}_{lo,n}
as in (
7.16
) for
n=1,3,5
. We estimate, using (
7.14
),

|II_{n}|\lesssim\left|\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\chi_{0}\left(\frac{\xi-\xi_{0}}{R}\right)e^{i\Phi_{\pm}(t,\xi,r)}\widetilde{\phi}_{n}(\xi)W_{\pm,n}(\xi,r)\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\right|\lesssim\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{R}{\langle\xi_{0}r\rangle^{1/2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2/3}}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

and

\displaystyle|III_{1}|\lesssim\|\partial_{\xi}(\xi\widetilde{\phi}_{n})\|_{L^{2+\alpha}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}\|\frac{1}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}t(\xi^{2}-\xi_{0}^{2})|\xi|^{\frac{1}{2+\alpha}}}\|_{L^{\frac{2+\alpha}{1+\alpha}}(\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lesssim\|\partial_{\xi}\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{2+\alpha}}\frac{1}{t^{\frac{3}{2}}\xi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2+\alpha}}R^{1-\frac{1+\alpha}{2+\alpha}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2/3+\frac{\alpha}{3(2+\alpha)}}}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{H^{1+\epsilon}}

\displaystyle|III_{n,2}|\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\xi_{0}+R}^{\xi_{0}+2R}\frac{1}{\xi^{2}-\xi_{0}^{2}}\frac{1}{R\langle\xi r\rangle^{1/2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2/3}}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle|III_{n,3}|\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\xi>\xi_{0}+CR}\frac{1}{\xi^{2}-\xi_{0}^{2}}\frac{\langle r\rangle}{\langle\xi r\rangle^{3/2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2/3}}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle|III_{n,4}|\lesssim\|\xi\widetilde{\phi}_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\xi>\xi_{0}+CR}\frac{\xi}{t(\xi^{2}-\xi_{0}^{2})^{2}}\frac{1}{\langle r\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2/3}}\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}}

where we used the Hölder inequality and the Sobolev embedding
H^{\epsilon}\subset L^{2+\alpha}
for some
\alpha>0
for the first inequality. This outline concludes the proof of (
7.8
).
∎

7.4.
End the proof of Theorem
1.1

Proof.

If
\phi\in L^{2,s}(r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r)
for some
s\in(1,3)
then we have
\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{H^{s}(|\xi|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi)}\lesssim\|\phi\|_{L^{2,s}}
by Lemma
5.3
. The desired
L^{2}
bounds for
e^{it\mathcal{H}}\phi
are then direct consequences of Theorem
7.1
, and the desired
L^{\infty}
bounds are consequences of Theorem
7.2
.
∎

8.
Jost solutions

The rest of this article is dedicated to the construction and description of the generalized eigenfunctions
\psi(\xi,r)
.
We will henceforth only consider the case
\xi\geqslant 0
(i.e.
\lambda\geqslant 0
)
and then define
\psi(-\xi,r)=-\sigma_{1}\psi(\xi,r)
.

The aim of this section is to obtain solutions with prescribed behavior in a neighborhood of both endpoints:
0
and
\infty
. This is easily achieved close to the origin, but at infinity, where the corresponding functions are called Jost solutions, the construction is more delicate. We construct Jost solutions which are either exponentially decaying or oscillating at infinity, and we also obtain quantitative bounds for
\lambda>\lambda_{0}>0
, for any
\lambda_{0}>0
that will be used to describe the limit
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty.
.

8.1.
Equivalent formulations of
\mathcal{H}-\lambda=0

We will study the ordinary differential equation

(8.1)

\mathcal{H}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u\\
v\end{array}\right)=\lambda\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u\\
v\end{array}\right)

where

\mathcal{H}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-\Delta+\frac{1}{r^{2}}+1&1\\
-1&\Delta-\frac{1}{r^{2}}-1\end{array}\right)+(\rho^{2}-1)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2&1\\
-1&-2\end{array}\right)

with
\Delta=\partial_{r}^{2}+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}
and
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
. Since
\sigma_{1}\mathcal{H}\sigma_{1}=-\mathcal{H}
, we focus on the case
\lambda\geqslant 0
. Recalling that

L_{0}=\Delta-\frac{1}{r^{2}}+(1-\rho^{2}),

we have

\mathcal{H}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-L_{0}+\rho^{2}&\rho^{2}\\
-\rho^{2}&L_{0}-\rho^{2}\end{array}\right).

The following lemma gives equivalent formulations of the equation (
8.1
).

Lemma 8.1
.

For
\lambda\geqslant 0
, the functions
(u,v)
solve
(
8.1
) if and only if

(8.2)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}L_{0}(u-v)=-\lambda(u+v)\\
(L_{0}-2\rho^{2})(u+v)=-\lambda(u-v).\end{array}\right.

Furthermore, with

\displaystyle a_{\pm}(\lambda)=\lambda\pm\langle\lambda\rangle

\displaystyle\xi^{2}=\langle\lambda\rangle-1=-\lambda-1+a_{+}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\kappa^{2}=1+\langle\lambda\rangle=\lambda+1-a_{-}(\lambda)\geqslant 2

this can also be written in the variables

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\varphi\\
\psi\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{u}{a_{+}(\lambda)}+v\\
-u-a_{-}(\lambda)v\end{array}\right)

as

(8.3)

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}(\Delta-\kappa^{2})\varphi\\
(\Delta+\xi^{2})\psi\end{array}\right)+V_{\lambda}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\varphi\\
\psi\end{array}\right)=0

with

V_{\lambda}=-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\operatorname{Id}-\frac{\rho^{2}(r)-1}{\langle\lambda\rangle}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1+2\langle\lambda\rangle&-\lambda\\
-\lambda&-1+2\langle\lambda\rangle\end{array}\right).

The choice of the variables
\varphi,\psi
is such that the potential

V_{\lambda}
converges to finite limits both when
\lambda\rightarrow 0
and

\lambda\rightarrow+\infty
(remark also that this is also true for

\frac{1}{a_{+}(\lambda)}
and
a_{-}(\lambda)
). The change of variables
(u,v)\rightarrow(\varphi,\psi)
is invertible for all
\lambda\geqslant 0
.

Proof.

If
(u,v)
solve (
8.1
), then

0=(\mathcal{H}-\lambda)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u\\
v\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-L_{0}(u)+\rho^{2}u-\lambda u+\rho^{2}v\\
L_{0}(v)-\rho^{2}v-\lambda v-\rho^{2}u\end{array}\right).

Summing and taking the difference of these two equalities yields
(
8.2
). It can also be written as

\left(\Delta-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u\\
v\end{array}\right)+\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-1+\lambda&-1\\
-1&-1-\lambda\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u\\
v\end{array}\right)-(\rho^{2}-1)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2&1\\
1&2\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u\\
v\end{array}\right)=0.

We check that

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-1+\lambda&-1\\
-1&-1-\lambda\end{array}\right)=P\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-\kappa^{2}&0\\
0&\xi^{2}\end{array}\right)P^{-1}

where

P=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-a_{-}(\lambda)&-a_{+}(\lambda)\\
1&1\end{array}\right)

and

P^{-1}=\frac{1}{2\langle\lambda\rangle}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&a_{+}(\lambda)\\
-1&-a_{-}(\lambda)\end{array}\right).

Using
a_{\pm}^{2}(\lambda)=1+2\lambda a_{\pm}(\lambda),a_{+}(\lambda)+a_{-}(\lambda)=2\lambda
and
a_{+}(\lambda)a_{-}(\lambda)=-1
, we
compute that

P^{-1}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2&1\\
1&2\end{array}\right)P=\frac{1}{\langle\lambda\rangle}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1+2\langle\lambda\rangle&-\lambda a_{+}(\lambda)\\
\lambda a_{-}(\lambda)&-1+2\langle\lambda\rangle\end{array}\right)

Therefore, since
a_{+}(\lambda)=\lambda+\langle\lambda\rangle\neq 0
for

\lambda\geqslant 0
, for the variables

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\varphi\\
\psi\end{array}\right)=2\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\langle\lambda\rangle}{a_{+}(\lambda)}&0\\
0&\langle\lambda\rangle\end{array}\right)P^{-1}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u\\
v\end{array}\right),

we get the desired equation.
∎

8.2.
Solutions near
r=0

Lemma 8.2
.

For any
\lambda\geqslant 0
, the four-dimensional

\mathbb{C}
-vector space
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
of solutions of

\mathcal{H}-\lambda=0
on
\mathbb{R}^{+\ast}
can be decomposed as

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}=\mathcal{E}_{1}+\mathcal{E}_{2}

where
\dim(\mathcal{E}_{1})=\dim(\mathcal{E}_{2})=2
, and for any
(u_{1},v_{1})\in\mathcal{E}_{1}
, there exists
(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
such that

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{1}\\
v_{1}\end{array}\right)=r\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\kappa_{1}\\
\kappa_{2}\end{array}\right)+O_{r\rightarrow 0}(r^{2})

and for any
(u_{2},v_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{2}
, there exists
(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
such that

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{2}\\
v_{2}\end{array}\right)=\frac{1}{r}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\kappa_{1}\\
\kappa_{2}\end{array}\right)+O_{r\rightarrow 0}(1).

Proof.

Since
L_{0}=\Delta-\frac{1}{r^{2}}+(1-\rho^{2})
and

(\mathcal{H}-\lambda)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u\\
v\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-L_{0}+\rho^{2}-\lambda&\rho^{2}\\
-\rho^{2}&L_{0}-\rho^{2}-\lambda\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u\\
v\end{array}\right),

we write the equation
(\mathcal{H}-\lambda)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u\\
v\end{array}\right)=0
as

\left(\Delta-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u\\
v\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}S_{1}(u,v)\\
S_{2}(u,v)\end{array}\right)

where

S_{1}(u,v)=\rho^{2}(2u+v)-(1+\lambda)u

and

S_{2}(u,v)=\rho^{2}(2v+u)-(1-\lambda)v.

The solutions of
\Delta-\frac{1}{r^{2}}=0
on
\mathbb{R}^{+\ast}
are spanned by
r
and
\frac{1}{r}
. Furthermore, a solution of
\left(\Delta-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\right)(u)=S
is

u(r)=r\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{1}{s^{3}}\int_{0}^{s}t^{2}S(t)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s

for any
r_{0}>0
.

We define the sequences of functions
(u_{n},v_{n})
by

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{0}\\
v_{0}\end{array}\right)(r)=r\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\end{array}\right)

and then

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{n+1}\\
v_{n+1}\end{array}\right)(r)=r\int_{0}^{r}\frac{1}{s^{3}}\int_{0}^{s}t^{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}S_{1}(u_{n},v_{n})\\
S_{2}(u_{n},v_{n})\end{array}\right)(t)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s.

Let us show that for
R_{0}>0
small enough and
n\geqslant 0
,

\left\|\frac{|u_{n+1}|+|v_{n+1}|}{r^{2}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([0,R_{0}])}\lesssim_{\lambda}R_{0}\left\|\frac{|u_{n}|+|v_{n}|}{r}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([0,R_{0}])}.

Indeed, we have that for
r\in[0,R_{0}]
,

\left|\left(\begin{array}[]{c}S_{1}(u_{n},v_{n})\\
S_{2}(u_{n},v_{n})\end{array}\right)(t)\right|\lesssim_{\lambda}t\left\|\frac{|u_{n}|+|v_{n}|}{r}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([0,R_{0}])}

and therefore

\displaystyle\left|r\int_{0}^{r}\frac{1}{s^{3}}\int_{0}^{s}t^{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}S_{1}(u_{n},v_{n})\\
S_{2}(u_{n},v_{n})\end{array}\right)(t)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\right|\lesssim_{\lambda}\left|r\int_{0}^{r}\frac{1}{s^{3}}\int_{0}^{s}t^{3}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\right|\left\|\frac{|u_{n}|+|v_{n}|}{r}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([0,R_{0}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim_{\lambda}r^{3}\left\|\frac{|u_{n}|+|v_{n}|}{r}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([0,R_{0}])}\lesssim_{\lambda}R_{0}r^{2}\left\|\frac{|u_{n}|+|v_{n}|}{r}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([0,R_{0}])},

leading to the result. Taking
R_{0}
small enough, we deduce that
(u_{n},v_{n})\rightarrow(u_{n+1},v_{n+1})
is a contraction for the norm
\left\|\frac{.}{r}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([0,R_{0}])}
, and thus

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u\\
v\end{array}\right)(r)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{n}\\
v_{n}\end{array}\right)(r)

is well-defined on
[0,R_{0}]
and solves
(\mathcal{H}-\lambda)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u\\
v\end{array}\right)=0
with

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u\\
v\end{array}\right)(r)=r\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\end{array}\right)+O_{r\rightarrow 0}(r^{2}).

A similar proof can be made to construct
(u,v)
such that

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u\\
v\end{array}\right)(r)=r\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
1\end{array}\right)+O_{r\rightarrow 0}(r^{2}),

and these functions form a basis of
\mathcal{E}_{1}
. Now, we construct
similarly a sequence of function on
]0,R_{0}[
by

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{0}\\
v_{0}\end{array}\right)(r)=\frac{1}{r}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\end{array}\right)

with

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{n+1}\\
v_{n+1}\end{array}\right)(r)=r\int_{R_{0}}^{r}\frac{1}{s^{3}}\int_{0}^{s}t^{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}S_{1}(u_{n},v_{n})\\
S_{2}(u_{n},v_{n})\end{array}\right)(t)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s.

We check that for
r\in]0,R_{0}[
,

\displaystyle\left|r\int_{R_{0}}^{r}\frac{1}{s^{3}}\int_{0}^{s}t^{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}S_{1}(u_{n},v_{n})\\
S_{2}(u_{n},v_{n})\end{array}\right)(t)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s\right|\lesssim_{\lambda}\left(r\int_{R_{0}}^{r}\frac{1}{s^{3}}\int_{0}^{s}t\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s\right)\|r(|u_{n}|+|v_{n}|)\|_{L^{\infty}([0,R_{0}])}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lesssim_{\lambda}KR_{0}|\ln(R_{0})|\|r(|u_{n}|+|v_{n}|)\|_{L^{\infty}([0,R_{0}])}

and thus as previously,
(u_{n},v_{n})\rightarrow(u_{n+1},v_{n+1})
is a
contraction for the norm
\|r.\|_{L^{\infty}([0,R_{0}])}
if
R_{0}>0
is
small enough, leading to

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u\\
v\end{array}\right)(r)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u_{n}\\
v_{n}\end{array}\right)(r)

being well-defined on
]0,R_{0}[
and solving
(\mathcal{H}-\lambda)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u\\
v\end{array}\right)=0
with

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u\\
v\end{array}\right)(r)=\frac{1}{r}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\end{array}\right)+O_{r\rightarrow 0}(1).

A similar construction can be done to have a solution satisfying

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}u\\
v\end{array}\right)(r)=\frac{1}{r}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
1\end{array}\right)+O_{r\rightarrow 0}(1)

and these two functions form a base of
\mathcal{E}_{2}
.
∎

8.3.
Preliminaries to the construction of Jost Solutions

For
\lambda>0
, recall that
\xi=\sqrt{\langle\lambda\rangle-1}>0
, and we define the change of variables

y=\xi r.

Recall that

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\varphi\\
\psi\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{u}{a_{+}(\lambda)}+v\\
-u-a_{-}(\lambda)v\end{array}\right).

Abusing notations and denoting
(\varphi,\psi)
for the rescaled functions through
y=\xi r
, we see that
(u,v)
is in the kernel of
\mathcal{H}-\lambda
if and only if

(8.4)

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\left(\Delta-\left(\frac{\kappa}{\xi}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{y^{2}}\right)\varphi\\
\left(\Delta+1-\frac{1}{y^{2}}\right)\psi\end{array}\right)(y)=\widetilde{V}_{\lambda}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\varphi\\
\psi\end{array}\right)(y)

with

(8.5)

\widetilde{V}_{\lambda}(y)=\frac{\frac{1}{\xi^{2}}\left(\rho^{2}\left(\frac{y}{\xi}\right)-1\right)}{\langle\lambda\rangle}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1+2\langle\lambda\rangle&-\lambda\\
-\lambda&-1+2\langle\lambda\rangle\end{array}\right).

We start with some estimates when
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty
for the coefficients in this equation and change of variables.

Lemma 8.3
.

For any
\lambda_{0}>0
, we have that for any
y\geqslant 1,\lambda\geqslant\lambda_{0},j,k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\left|\partial_{\lambda}^{j}\left(\frac{\kappa}{\xi}-1\right)\right|+|\partial_{\lambda}^{j}a_{-}(\lambda)|+\left|\partial_{\lambda}^{j}\left(\frac{1}{a_{+}}\right)(\lambda)\right|\lesssim_{\lambda_{0},j}\frac{1}{\lambda^{1+j}}

and

|\partial_{\lambda}^{j}\partial_{y}^{k}\widetilde{V}_{\lambda}|\lesssim_{\lambda_{0},j,k}\frac{1}{(\xi+y)^{2}}\frac{1}{\lambda^{j}(1+y)^{k}}.

Proof.

By expanding the formulas

\displaystyle\frac{1}{a_{+}(\lambda)}=\frac{1}{\lambda}\left(\frac{1}{1+\sqrt{1+\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}}}\right)

\displaystyle a_{-}(\lambda)=\lambda\left(1-\sqrt{1+\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\kappa}{\xi}\right)^{2}=1+2\left(\frac{1+\sqrt{1+\lambda^{2}}}{\lambda^{2}}\right),

the first assertion of the lemma follows easily.
We check similarly that for

\lambda\geqslant\lambda_{0}
,

\left|\partial_{\lambda}^{j}\left(\frac{1}{\langle\lambda\rangle}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1+2\langle\lambda\rangle&-\lambda\\
-\lambda&-1+2\langle\lambda\rangle\end{array}\right)\right)\right|\lesssim_{\lambda_{0},j}\frac{1}{\lambda^{j}}.

Therefore, there only remains to show that

\left|\partial_{\lambda}^{j}\partial_{y}^{k}\left[\frac{1}{\xi^{2}}\left(\rho^{2}\left(\frac{y}{\xi}\right)-1\right)\right]\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{(\xi+y)^{2}\lambda^{j}y^{k}}

Since
|\partial_{\lambda}^{j}\xi^{\prime}(\lambda)|\lesssim\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}-j}
for
j\in\mathbb{N}
, it suffices to show that

\left|\partial_{\xi}^{j}\partial_{y}^{k}\left[\frac{1}{\xi^{2}}\left(\rho^{2}\left(\frac{y}{\xi}\right)-1\right)\right]\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{(\xi^{2}+y^{2})\xi^{j}y^{k}}.

Denoting
R_{k}
for the
k
-th derivative of
\rho^{2}-1
, we know by Lemma
3.1
that
|R_{k}(z)|\lesssim\langle z\rangle^{-2-k}
and the above left-hand side can be written
\left|\partial_{\xi}^{j}\left[\xi^{-2-k}R_{k}\left(\frac{y}{\xi}\right)\right]\right|
. Applying Leibniz’ rule to this expression, we obtain a sum of terms of the type

\xi^{-2-k-j}Z\left(\frac{y}{\xi}\right)
, where
Z(y)\lesssim\langle z\rangle^{-2-k}
. Since

\xi^{-2-k-j}\frac{1}{1+\left(\frac{y}{\xi}\right)^{2+k}}\lesssim\frac{1}{(\xi^{2}+y^{2})\xi^{j}y^{k}},

we obtain the desired result.
∎

From now on, we will use the notations of Annex
A
that we
recall here. In the rest of this section, given some
\lambda_{0}>0
, we will
use for
n,m,b,c\in\mathbb{N}
the norm

\mathcal{N}_{b,c}^{n,m}(f)=\sum_{j=0}^{n}\sum_{k=0}^{n+m-j}\|y^{b}(y+\xi)^{c}y^{k}\lambda^{j}\partial_{y}^{k}\partial_{\lambda}^{j}f\|_{L^{\infty}([1,+\infty(\times[\lambda_{0},+\infty()}

which is the one studied in Annex
A
for the parameters
\nu=\xi,r_{0}=1
(taken now in the variable
y
instead of
r
). This norm
measure functions that decays like
\frac{1}{y^{b}(y+\xi)^{c}}
when
y\rightarrow+\infty
and for which taking any combinaison of
n
times or
less of either
y\partial_{y}
or
\lambda\partial_{\lambda}
, and up to an
additional
m
times
y\partial_{y}
derivatives, does not change this decay.
Since
\xi(\lambda)\rightarrow+\infty
when
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty
, although for instance
\mathcal{N}_{0,1}^{0,0}(f)\sim\mathcal{N}_{1,0}^{0,0}(f)
, the equivalence constant is not independent of

\lambda
.

Remark that by Lemma
8.3
, for any
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
,

(8.6)

\mathcal{N}_{0,2}^{n,m}(\widetilde{V}_{\lambda})\lesssim_{n,m}1.

Still from Annex
A
, for
b\in\mathbb{Z},x>0
we define the
space
\mathcal{W}_{b}(x^{+})
as the set of functions
f:[x,+\infty(\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that
\|r^{b}r^{k}\partial_{r}^{k}f\|_{L^{\infty}([x,+\infty()}<+\infty
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\mathcal{W}_{b}(1^{+},\lambda_{0}^{+})
the set of functions
f:[1,+\infty(\times[\lambda_{0},+\infty(\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that
\|r^{b}r^{k}\lambda^{j}\partial_{\lambda}^{j}\partial_{r}^{k}f\|_{L^{\infty}([1,+\infty(\times[\lambda_{0},+\infty()}<+\infty
for all
j,k\in\mathbb{N}
. Remark for instance that, by Lemma
8.3
we have
\frac{\kappa}{\xi}\in 1+\mathcal{W}_{1}(\lambda_{0}^{+})
for any
\lambda_{0}>0
.

Finally , given a function
a
, we define the operator
D_{a}=\partial_{y}+a
,
and a choice of inverse
D_{a}^{-1}
depending on properties of
a
, given in
Definition
A.4
.

8.4.
The exponentially decaying solution near
+\infty

Lemma 8.4
.

For any
\lambda>0
, there exists a solution of
(
8.4
) of the form

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\varphi_{2}\\
\psi_{2}\end{array}\right)=K_{1}\left(\frac{\kappa}{\xi}\cdot\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{array}\right)

such that, for any
\lambda_{0}>0
, taking
\lambda\geqslant\lambda_{0}
and

y\geqslant 1
, we have for any
j,k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\left|\partial_{\lambda}^{j}\partial_{y}^{k}\left(\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{array}\right)-\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\end{array}\right)\right)\right|\lesssim_{\lambda_{0},j,k}\frac{1}{(\xi+y)\lambda^{j}y^{k}}.

Furthermore, given
y_{0}\geqslant 1
, we have

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\varphi_{2}\\
\varphi_{2}^{\prime}\\
\psi_{2}\\
\psi_{2}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)(y_{0})=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}K_{1}\left(y_{0}\right)\\
K_{1}^{\prime}\left(y_{0}\right)\\
0\\
0\end{array}\right)+o_{\lambda\rightarrow+\infty}(1)

and for any
k\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
,

\left|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\varphi_{2}\\
\varphi_{2}^{\prime}\\
\psi_{2}\\
\psi_{2}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)(y_{0})\right|\lesssim_{\lambda_{0},y_{0},k}\frac{1}{\xi\lambda^{k}}.

Proof.

We fix some
\lambda_{0}>0
and consider
\lambda\geqslant\lambda_{0}
. We
recall that equation (
8.4
) is

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\left(\Delta-\left(\frac{\kappa}{\xi}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{y^{2}}\right)\varphi\\
\left(\Delta+1-\frac{1}{y^{2}}\right)\psi\end{array}\right)=\widetilde{V}_{\lambda}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\varphi\\
\psi\end{array}\right).

We look for a solution of the form

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\varphi\\
\psi\end{array}\right)=K_{1}\left(\frac{\kappa}{\xi}.\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{array}\right).

Since

\left(\Delta-\left(\frac{\kappa}{\xi}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{y^{2}}\right)\left(K_{1}\left(\frac{\kappa}{\xi}.\right)\right)=0,

dividing the equation by
K_{1}\left(\frac{\kappa}{\xi}.\right)
we get

(8.7)

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h^{\prime\prime}_{1}+\left(2\frac{\kappa}{\xi}\frac{K_{1}^{\prime}}{K_{1}}\left(\frac{\kappa}{\xi}.\right)+\frac{1}{y}\right)h_{1}^{\prime}\\
h^{\prime\prime}_{2}+\left(2\frac{\kappa}{\xi}\frac{K_{1}^{\prime}}{K_{1}}\left(\frac{\kappa}{\xi}.\right)+\frac{1}{y}\right)h_{2}^{\prime}+\left(1+\left(\frac{\kappa}{\xi}\right)^{2}\right)h_{2}\end{array}\right)=\widetilde{V}_{\lambda}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{array}\right).

Recalling the definition of the operator
D_{a}(h)=h^{\prime}+ah
, we have with

a(y,\lambda)=2\frac{\kappa}{\xi}\frac{K_{1}^{\prime}}{K_{1}}\left(\frac{\kappa}{\xi}.\right)+\frac{1}{y},

that

h^{\prime\prime}_{1}+\left(2\frac{\kappa}{\xi}\frac{K_{1}^{\prime}}{K_{1}}\left(\frac{\kappa}{\xi}.\right)+\frac{1}{y}\right)h_{1}^{\prime}=D_{a}D_{0}(h).

By Lemma
B.2
and
\lambda\rightarrow\frac{\kappa}{\xi}\in\mathcal{W}_{0}(\lambda_{0}^{+})
from Lemma
8.3
, we have

a\in-2\frac{\kappa}{\xi}+\mathcal{W}_{2}(1^{+},\lambda_{0}^{+})

and is therefore of type
<0
in Definition
A.3
(since

\frac{\kappa}{\xi}\rightarrow 1
when
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty
).

We now look for
b_{1},b_{2}
such that

D_{b_{1}}D_{b_{2}}(h_{2})=h_{2}^{\prime\prime}+ah_{2}^{\prime}+\left(1+\left(\frac{\kappa}{\xi}\right)^{2}\right)h_{2}.

which leads to the system

b_{1}+b_{2}=a,\qquad b_{2}^{\prime}=b_{2}^{2}-ab_{2}+1+\left(\frac{\kappa}{\xi}\right)^{2}.

We notice that then two solutions of
D_{b_{1}}D_{b_{2}}=0
would be
h_{2\pm}=\frac{J_{1}\pm iY_{1}}{K_{1}(\frac{\kappa}{\xi}\cdot)}
. We choose
b_{2}=-\frac{h_{2+}^{\prime}}{h_{2+}}
, which is well defined as
h_{2+}
does not vanish, and which produces
D_{b_{2}}h_{2+}=0
. Then
D_{b_{2}}h_{2-}=(h_{2-}^{\prime}h_{2+}-h_{2+}^{\prime}h_{2-})/h_{2+}
which does not vanish as we recognize the Wronskian between
h_{2-}
and
h_{2+}
. We then define
b_{1}=-\frac{(D_{b_{2}}h_{2+})^{\prime}}{D_{b_{2}}h_{2+}}
. Notice then that
h_{2+}
and
h_{2-}
are solutions to
D_{b_{1}}D_{b_{2}}=0
, so that necessarily
b_{1}
and
b_{2}
satisfy the system above, hence
b_{1}=a-b_{2}
. We compute

(8.8)

b_{2}=\frac{-(J_{1}+iY_{1})^{\prime}}{J_{1}+iY_{1}}+\frac{\kappa}{\xi}\frac{K_{1}^{\prime}}{K_{1}}\left(\frac{\kappa}{\xi}\cdot\right)=i-\frac{\kappa}{\xi}+\mathcal{W}_{2,0}(1^{+},\lambda_{0}^{+})

by Lemma
B.2
.3 and
\lambda\rightarrow\frac{\kappa}{\xi}\in\mathcal{W}_{0}(\lambda_{0}^{+})
. This gives in turn

b_{1}=a-b_{2}=-i-\frac{\kappa}{\xi}+\mathcal{W}_{2,0}(1^{+},\lambda_{0}^{+}).

Equation (
8.7
) is now

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}D_{a}(D_{0}(h_{1}))\\
D_{b_{1}}(D_{b_{2}}(h_{2}))\end{array}\right)=\widetilde{V}_{\lambda}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{array}\right).

Since
D_{0}(1)=0
, we look for a solution of the form

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\end{array}\right)+\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}D_{0}^{-1}D_{a}^{-1}&0\\
0&D_{b_{2}}^{-1}D_{b_{1}}^{-1}\end{array}\right)\left(\widetilde{V}_{\lambda}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{array}\right)\right).

Defining the operator

\Theta=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}D_{0}^{-1}D_{a}^{-1}&0\\
0&D_{b_{2}}^{-1}D_{b_{1}}^{-1}\end{array}\right)(\widetilde{V}_{\lambda}.),

we want to construct

(8.9)

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{array}\right)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\Theta^{k}\left(\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\end{array}\right)\right).

We have shown that
a,b_{1},b_{2}
are all of type
<0
in Definition

A.3
. By Proposition
A.1
and (
8.6
), we deduce
that for any
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
, we have for
\lambda\geqslant\lambda_{0}
that

\mathcal{N}_{0,1}^{n,m}\left(D_{0}^{-1}D_{a}^{-1}\left(\widetilde{V}_{\lambda}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{array}\right)\right)\right)\lesssim_{\lambda_{0},n,m}\mathcal{N}_{0,0}^{n,m}(h_{1})+\mathcal{N}_{0,0}^{n,m}(h_{2})

and

\mathcal{N}_{0,2}^{n,m}\left(D_{b_{2}}^{-1}D_{b_{1}}^{-1}\left(\widetilde{V}_{\lambda}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{array}\right)\right)\right)\lesssim_{\lambda_{0},n,m}\mathcal{N}_{0,0}^{n,m}(h_{1})+\mathcal{N}_{0,0}^{n,m}(h_{2}).

In particular, for
n=m=0
, this means that for any
R\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\left\|\Theta\left(\begin{pmatrix}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{pmatrix}\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}([R,+\infty(\times[\lambda_{0},+\infty()}\lesssim_{\lambda_{0}}\frac{1}{R+\lambda_{0}}\left\|\left(\begin{pmatrix}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{pmatrix}\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}([R,+\infty(\times[\lambda_{0},+\infty()},

\displaystyle\left\|\Theta\left(\begin{pmatrix}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{pmatrix}\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}([1,+\infty(\times[R,+\infty()}\lesssim_{\lambda_{0}}\frac{1}{1+R}\left\|\left(\begin{pmatrix}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{pmatrix}\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}([1,+\infty(\times[R,+\infty()},

and therefore
\Theta
is a contraction for these norms provided that
R\geqslant 1
is large enough (uniformly in
\lambda\geqslant\lambda_{0}
).
This completes the construction of
(h_{1},h_{2})
on
(y,\lambda_{0})\in[R,+\infty)\times[\lambda_{0},\infty)\cup[1,+\infty)\times[R,\infty)
by
(
8.9
).

Similarly, for any
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
,
\Theta
is a contraction for the
norm
\mathcal{N}_{0,0}^{n,m}
if we restrict to sets of the
form
[R_{n,m},+\infty)\times[\lambda_{0},+\infty)\cup[1,+\infty)\times[R_{n,m},+\infty)
. This gives the required estimates on

(h_{1},h_{2})
on this set.

Now, we can extend this construction to the set
(y,\lambda)\in[1,R_{n,m}\times[\lambda_{0},R_{n,m}]]
by Cauchy theory, so that
(h_{1},h_{2})
is defined on the whole set
(y,\lambda)\in[1,+\infty)\times[\lambda_{0},\infty)
. Indeed, this is simply because for all
\lambda\in[\lambda_{0},R_{n,m}]
, the function
(h_{1}(\cdot,\lambda),h_{2}(\cdot,\lambda))
solves on
[R_{n,m},\infty)
the differential
equation (
8.7
), for which all coefficients are smooth, so that the regularity of
(h_{1},h_{2})
extends to
[1,R_{n,m}\times[\lambda_{0},R_{n,m}]]
.

This completes the proof of the existence of
(h_{1},h_{2})
solving
(
8.7
) with

\mathcal{N}_{0,0}^{n,m}\left(\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{array}\right)\right)\lesssim_{n,m}1

for any
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
. But since

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\end{array}\right)+\Theta\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{array}\right),

with the properties shown on
\Theta
, we have in fact that

(8.10)

\mathcal{N}_{0,1}^{n,m}(h_{1}-1)+\mathcal{N}_{0,2}^{n,m}(h_{2})\lesssim_{n,m,\lambda_{0}}1,

which completes the proof of

\left|\partial_{\lambda}^{j}\partial_{y}^{k}\left(\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{array}\right)-\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\end{array}\right)\right)\right|\lesssim_{j,k,\lambda_{0}}\frac{1}{(y+\xi)y^{k}\lambda^{j}}

for
y\geqslant 1,\lambda\geqslant\lambda_{0}
and any
j,k\in\mathbb{N}
. In particular, since
\xi\rightarrow+\infty
when
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty
, we have

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\end{array}\right)+o_{\lambda\rightarrow+\infty}(1)

and since
\frac{\kappa}{\xi}\rightarrow 1
when
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty
by Lemma
8.3
, we have for
y_{0}\geqslant 1
that

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\varphi_{2}\\
\psi_{2}\end{array}\right)(y_{0})=K_{1}\left(\frac{\kappa}{\xi}y_{0}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{array}\right)(y_{0})=K_{1}(y_{0})\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\end{array}\right)+o_{\lambda\rightarrow+\infty}(1).

We obtain the estimates on
\varphi_{2}^{\prime}(y_{0}),\psi_{2}^{\prime}(y_{0})
similarly. The
estimates on their derivatives with respect to
\lambda
are a consequence
of
\frac{\kappa}{\xi}-1\in\mathcal{W}_{1}(\lambda_{0}^{+})
and
(
8.10
).
∎

8.5.
The oscillating solutions near
+\infty

Lemma 8.5
.

For any
\lambda>0
, there exists two real valued solutions
of (
8.4
) of the form

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\varphi_{3}\\
\psi_{3}\end{array}\right)+i\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\varphi_{4}\\
\psi_{4}\end{array}\right)=(J_{1}+iY_{1})\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{array}\right)

such that, for any
\lambda_{0}>0
, taking
\lambda\geqslant\lambda_{0}
and

y\geqslant 1
, we have that for any
j,k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\left|\partial_{\lambda}^{j}\partial_{y}^{k}\left(\left(\begin{array}[]{c}h_{1}\\
h_{2}\end{array}\right)-\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
1\end{array}\right)\right)\right|\lesssim_{\lambda_{0},j,k}\frac{1}{(\xi+y)\lambda^{j}y^{k}}.
